[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... I came in because people keep telling me I'm imagining things, but I'm not. The hospital people, and some folks from the agency where I get my cleaning work, they've been watching our house and my routes, like checking if I'm taking proper care of my husband. And I do take care of him, I always have—same way I keep a house, you know, you notice every little thing when you clean for a living. But they act like I don't see them watching.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually seen these people watching you or is it more of a feeling that you're being watched?\n\nPatient: I’ve seen enough to know it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling. They act like regular people passing by, but I notice patterns—cars near the house, people around my cleaning routes, and the hospital people asking questions like they already know things. And I do take care of him, I always have—same way I keep a house, you know, you notice every little thing when you clean for a living. But they act like I don't see them watching.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually seen these people watching you or is it more of a feeling that you're being watched?\n\nPatient: I’ve seen enough to know it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling. They act like regular people passing by, but I notice patterns—cars near the house, people around my cleaning routes, and the hospital people asking questions like they already know things. When you clean houses for years, you learn what belongs and what doesn’t, you know? And now they’re acting like I’m making it up, but I know they’re watching to see if I’m taking proper care of my husband.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes... I mean, when the house is quiet, especially by the kitchen, I hear a woman’s voice near the window. Clear, like she’s standing right there, telling me to check the locks or move my husband’s pills. And I know how sounds carry in a house—I’ve cleaned enough places to know pipes, neighbors, all that—but this isn’t like that. It’s right there by the kitchen window.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by patterns, and how you connect those to people watching you?\n\nPatient: It’s like… when the same kind of car is sitting near our house more than once, or I see somebody from the agency around where I’m cleaning, not where they usually would be. People think I don’t notice, but I do. I’ve cleaned houses a long time, and you learn when something’s been moved or when somebody’s been somewhere they shouldn’t.\n\nAnd then the hospital people ask things about my husband, about whether I’m taking care of him right, like they already have been checking. That’s what I mean by patterns. It all connects. They’re watching the house and my routes, trying to see if I’m doing what I’m supposed to do. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. It’s about my husband, whether I’m taking proper care of him. And I’ve worked in people’s homes for years, you notice details—like if someone’s been in a room, or if a car is there too often, or people show up where they don’t belong. Well… step by step, it’s like, first my husband’s at the hospital and they start asking all these questions, like they already know things about our house. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.

About… I think about eleven months. Not quite a year, but close. That’s when I started noticing it more clearly, the watching and the things lining up.

And the voices too, around that same time. Our old place had pipes that knocked all the time, so I know the difference between normal house sounds and… anyway. But yes, about eleven months now.

I’ve cleaned houses a long time, and you learn when something’s been moved or when somebody’s been somewhere they shouldn’t.\n\nAnd then the hospital people ask things about my husband, about whether I’m taking care of him right, like they already have been checking. That’s what I mean by patterns. It all connects. They’re watching the house and my routes, trying to see if I’m doing what I’m supposed to do. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. It’s about my husband, whether I’m taking proper care of him. And I’ve worked in people’s homes for years, you notice details—like if someone’s been in a room, or if a car is there too often, or people show up where they don’t belong. So maybe other people can brush that off, but I can’t. That’s why I’m here, because they act like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you connect the hospital people, the agency, and the patterns you've noticed, in a step-by-step way?\n\nPatient: Well… step by step, it’s like, first my husband’s at the hospital and they start asking all these questions, like they already know things about our house. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.\n\nAnd I’ve cleaned houses for years, so I know when things don’t fit. Like one lady I used to clean for, she’d move every little figurine and then ask if I dusted right—so I notice details, you know? People think maids don’t see anything, but we see everything.\n\nSo the hospital people are worried, or pretending to be worried, about whether I’m taking proper care of my husband. And then some agency folks are around my routes, and it matches up. That’s why I say they’re watching the house and watching where I go. I’m here because people keep acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns and feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: About… I think about eleven months. Not quite a year, but close. That’s when I started noticing it more clearly, the watching and the things lining up.\n\nAnd the voices too, around that same time. I mean, it’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first you tell yourself maybe it’s nothing, like when you hear a noise in the house and you think it’s just the pipes or the neighbors. Our old place had pipes that knocked all the time, so I know the difference between normal house sounds and… anyway. But yes, about eleven months now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. It’s about my husband, whether I’m taking proper care of him. And I’ve worked in people’s homes for years, you notice details—like if someone’s been in a room, or if a car is there too often, or people show up where they don’t belong. So maybe other people can brush that off, but I can’t. That’s why I’m here, because they act like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you connect the hospital people, the agency, and the patterns you've noticed, in a step-by-step way?\n\nPatient: Well… step by step, it’s like, first my husband’s at the hospital and they start asking all these questions, like they already know things about our house. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.\n\nAnd I’ve cleaned houses for years, so I know when things don’t fit. Like one lady I used to clean for, she’d move every little figurine and then ask if I dusted right—so I notice details, you know? People think maids don’t see anything, but we see everything.\n\nSo the hospital people are worried, or pretending to be worried, about whether I’m taking proper care of my husband. And then some agency folks are around my routes, and it matches up. That’s why I say they’re watching the house and watching where I go. I’m here because people keep acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns and feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: About… I think about eleven months. Not quite a year, but close. That’s when I started noticing it more clearly, the watching and the things lining up.\n\nAnd the voices too, around that same time. I mean, it’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first you tell yourself maybe it’s nothing, like when you hear a noise in the house and you think it’s just the pipes or the neighbors. Our old place had pipes that knocked all the time, so I know the difference between normal house sounds and… anyway. But yes, about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without interrupting or going off on a tangent, just focusing on that one pattern?\n\nPatient: One pattern… okay, I can try. Then it connects to the church schedule, because certain people show up on the same days, and then the numbers on my client list—like house numbers, appointment times—and I know that sounds like I’m jumping around.\n\nPeople tell me they can’t follow me when I explain it. I mean, when the house is quiet, especially by the kitchen, I hear a woman’s voice near the window. Clear, like she’s standing right there, telling me to check the locks or move my husband’s pills. And I know how sounds carry in a house—I’ve cleaned enough places to know pipes, neighbors, all that—but this isn’t like that. It’s right there by the kitchen window.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by patterns, and how you connect those to people watching you?\n\nPatient: It’s like… when the same kind of car is sitting near our house more than once, or I see somebody from the agency around where I’m cleaning, not where they usually would be. People think I don’t notice, but I do. I’ve cleaned houses a long time, and you learn when something’s been moved or when somebody’s been somewhere they shouldn’t.\n\nAnd then the hospital people ask things about my husband, about whether I’m taking care of him right, like they already have been checking. That’s what I mean by patterns. It all connects. They’re watching the house and my routes, trying to see if I’m doing what I’m supposed to do. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. It’s about my husband, whether I’m taking proper care of him. And I’ve worked in people’s homes for years, you notice details—like if someone’s been in a room, or if a car is there too often, or people show up where they don’t belong. So maybe other people can brush that off, but I can’t. That’s why I’m here, because they act like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you connect the hospital people, the agency, and the patterns you've noticed, in a step-by-step way?\n\nPatient: Well… step by step, it’s like, first my husband’s at the hospital and they start asking all these questions, like they already know things about our house. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.\n\nAnd I’ve cleaned houses for years, so I know when things don’t fit. Like one lady I used to clean for, she’d move every little figurine and then ask if I dusted right—so I notice details, you know? People think maids don’t see anything, but we see everything.\n\nSo the hospital people are worried, or pretending to be worried, about whether I’m taking proper care of my husband. And then some agency folks are around my routes, and it matches up. That’s why I say they’re watching the house and watching where I go. I’m here because people keep acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns and feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: About… I think about eleven months. Not quite a year, but close. That’s when I started noticing it more clearly, the watching and the things lining up.\n\nAnd the voices too, around that same time. I mean, it’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first you tell yourself maybe it’s nothing, like when you hear a noise in the house and you think it’s just the pipes or the neighbors. Our old place had pipes that knocked all the time, so I know the difference between normal house sounds and… anyway. But yes, about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without interrupting or going off on a tangent, just focusing on that one pattern?\n\nPatient: One pattern… okay, I can try. It’s like when I start explaining it, I begin with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s what they keep looking at, whether I’m keeping track right. Then it connects to the church schedule, because certain people show up on the same days, and then the numbers on my client list—like house numbers, appointment times—and I know that sounds like I’m jumping around.\n\nPeople tell me they can’t follow me when I explain it. They say, “Stay on one thing,” but it all runs together in my head because it’s all part of the same pattern. I’m not trying to go off, I just… the words go from one piece to the next before I can slow them down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without explaining how it connects to anything else, just the pattern itself?\n\nPatient: That’s what I’m trying to say… the pattern itself is how my words go. I start with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s in my mind first, and then suddenly I’m talking about the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, like the house numbers or times.\n\nAnd I know you said not how it connects, but that’s where I get stuck, because in my head it doesn’t stay in one little box. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. They say I’m jumping around. I’m not trying to, it just runs from one thing to the next before I can stop it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But they act like I don't see them watching.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually seen these people watching you or is it more of a feeling that you're being watched?\n\nPatient: I’ve seen enough to know it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling. They act like regular people passing by, but I notice patterns—cars near the house, people around my cleaning routes, and the hospital people asking questions like they already know things. When you clean houses for years, you learn what belongs and what doesn’t, you know? And now they’re acting like I’m making it up, but I know they’re watching to see if I’m taking proper care of my husband.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes... I mean, when the house is quiet, especially by the kitchen, I hear a woman’s voice near the window. Clear, like she’s standing right there, telling me to check the locks or move my husband’s pills. And I know how sounds carry in a house—I’ve cleaned enough places to know pipes, neighbors, all that—but this isn’t like that. It’s right there by the kitchen window.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by patterns, and how you connect those to people watching you?\n\nPatient: It’s like… when the same kind of car is sitting near our house more than once, or I see somebody from the agency around where I’m cleaning, not where they usually would be. People think I don’t notice, but I do. I’ve cleaned houses a long time, and you learn when something’s been moved or when somebody’s been somewhere they shouldn’t.\n\nAnd then the hospital people ask things about my husband, about whether I’m taking care of him right, like they already have been checking. That’s what I mean by patterns. It all connects. They’re watching the house and my routes, trying to see if I’m doing what I’m supposed to do. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. It’s about my husband, whether I’m taking proper care of him. And I’ve worked in people’s homes for years, you notice details—like if someone’s been in a room, or if a car is there too often, or people show up where they don’t belong. So maybe other people can brush that off, but I can’t. That’s why I’m here, because they act like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you connect the hospital people, the agency, and the patterns you've noticed, in a step-by-step way?\n\nPatient: Well… step by step, it’s like, first my husband’s at the hospital and they start asking all these questions, like they already know things about our house. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.\n\nAnd I’ve cleaned houses for years, so I know when things don’t fit. Like one lady I used to clean for, she’d move every little figurine and then ask if I dusted right—so I notice details, you know? People think maids don’t see anything, but we see everything.\n\nSo the hospital people are worried, or pretending to be worried, about whether I’m taking proper care of my husband. And then some agency folks are around my routes, and it matches up. That’s why I say they’re watching the house and watching where I go. I’m here because people keep acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns and feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: About… I think about eleven months. Not quite a year, but close. That’s when I started noticing it more clearly, the watching and the things lining up.\n\nAnd the voices too, around that same time. I mean, it’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first you tell yourself maybe it’s nothing, like when you hear a noise in the house and you think it’s just the pipes or the neighbors. Our old place had pipes that knocked all the time, so I know the difference between normal house sounds and… anyway. But yes, about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without interrupting or going off on a tangent, just focusing on that one pattern?\n\nPatient: One pattern… okay, I can try. It’s like when I start explaining it, I begin with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s what they keep looking at, whether I’m keeping track right. Then it connects to the church schedule, because certain people show up on the same days, and then the numbers on my client list—like house numbers, appointment times—and I know that sounds like I’m jumping around.\n\nPeople tell me they can’t follow me when I explain it. They say, “Stay on one thing,” but it all runs together in my head because it’s all part of the same pattern. I’m not trying to go off, I just… the words go from one piece to the next before I can slow them down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without explaining how it connects to anything else, just the pattern itself?\n\nPatient: That’s what I’m trying to say… the pattern itself is how my words go. I start with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s in my mind first, and then suddenly I’m talking about the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, like the house numbers or times.\n\nAnd I know you said not how it connects, but that’s where I get stuck, because in my head it doesn’t stay in one little box. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. They say I’m jumping around. I’m not trying to, it just runs from one thing to the next before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me one thing that is happening to you right now, without adding any other details?\n\nPatient: My words are running together right now. I’m trying to keep it to one thing, but they keep wanting to go from the pills to the church schedule to my client numbers, and then people say they can’t follow me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF22, F23, F25\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... I came in because people keep telling me I'm imagining things, but I'm not. The hospital people, and some folks from the agency where I get my cleaning work, they've been watching our house and my routes, like checking if I'm taking proper care of my husband. And I do take care of him, I always have—same way I keep a house, you know, you notice every little thing when you clean for a living. But they act like I don't see them watching.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually seen these people watching you or is it more of a feeling that you're being watched?\n\nPatient: I’ve seen enough to know it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling. They act like regular people passing by, but I notice patterns—cars near the house, people around my cleaning routes, and the hospital people asking questions like they already know things. When you clean houses for years, you learn what belongs and what doesn’t, you know? And now they’re acting like I’m making it up, but I know they’re watching to see if I’m taking proper care of my husband.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes... I mean, when the house is quiet, especially by the kitchen, I hear a woman’s voice near the window. Clear, like she’s standing right there, telling me to check the locks or move my husband’s pills. And I know how sounds carry in a house—I’ve cleaned enough places to know pipes, neighbors, all that—but this isn’t like that. It’s right there by the kitchen window.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by patterns, and how you connect those to people watching you?\n\nPatient: It’s like… when the same kind of car is sitting near our house more than once, or I see somebody from the agency around where I’m cleaning, not where they usually would be. People think I don’t notice, but I do. I’ve cleaned houses a long time, and you learn when something’s been moved or when somebody’s been somewhere they shouldn’t.\n\nAnd then the hospital people ask things about my husband, about whether I’m taking care of him right, like they already have been checking. That’s what I mean by patterns. It all connects. They’re watching the house and my routes, trying to see if I’m doing what I’m supposed to do. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. It’s about my husband, whether I’m taking proper care of him. And I’ve worked in people’s homes for years, you notice details—like if someone’s been in a room, or if a car is there too often, or people show up where they don’t belong. So maybe other people can brush that off, but I can’t. That’s why I’m here, because they act like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you connect the hospital people, the agency, and the patterns you've noticed, in a step-by-step way?\n\nPatient: Well… step by step, it’s like, first my husband’s at the hospital and they start asking all these questions, like they already know things about our house. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.\n\nAnd I’ve cleaned houses for years, so I know when things don’t fit. Like one lady I used to clean for, she’d move every little figurine and then ask if I dusted right—so I notice details, you know? People think maids don’t see anything, but we see everything.\n\nSo the hospital people are worried, or pretending to be worried, about whether I’m taking proper care of my husband. And then some agency folks are around my routes, and it matches up. That’s why I say they’re watching the house and watching where I go. I’m here because people keep acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns and feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: About… I think about eleven months. Not quite a year, but close. That’s when I started noticing it more clearly, the watching and the things lining up.\n\nAnd the voices too, around that same time. I mean, it’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first you tell yourself maybe it’s nothing, like when you hear a noise in the house and you think it’s just the pipes or the neighbors. Our old place had pipes that knocked all the time, so I know the difference between normal house sounds and… anyway. But yes, about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without interrupting or going off on a tangent, just focusing on that one pattern?\n\nPatient: One pattern… okay, I can try. It’s like when I start explaining it, I begin with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s what they keep looking at, whether I’m keeping track right. Then it connects to the church schedule, because certain people show up on the same days, and then the numbers on my client list—like house numbers, appointment times—and I know that sounds like I’m jumping around.\n\nPeople tell me they can’t follow me when I explain it. They say, “Stay on one thing,” but it all runs together in my head because it’s all part of the same pattern. I’m not trying to go off, I just… the words go from one piece to the next before I can slow them down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without explaining how it connects to anything else, just the pattern itself?\n\nPatient: That’s what I’m trying to say… the pattern itself is how my words go. I start with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s in my mind first, and then suddenly I’m talking about the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, like the house numbers or times.\n\nAnd I know you said not how it connects, but that’s where I get stuck, because in my head it doesn’t stay in one little box. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. They say I’m jumping around. I’m not trying to, it just runs from one thing to the next before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me one thing that is happening to you right now, without adding any other details?\n\nPatient: My words are running together right now. I’m trying to keep it to one thing, but they keep wanting to go from the pills to the church schedule to my client numbers, and then people say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that happens a lot. I’ll try to answer one thing, and then my words start moving—like I’ll be talking about my husband’s heart pills, and then I’m already on the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, the house numbers or times.\n\nI don’t mean to do it. People tell me they can’t follow me, and I can see it on their faces sometimes, like I’ve lost them.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

They make sense to me while I’m saying them, sort of… but then when they come out, they get all tangled. Like I start trying to explain one thing, and my words go from my husband’s heart pills to the church schedule, and then to the numbers on my client list.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... I came in because people keep telling me I'm imagining things, but I'm not. The hospital people, and some folks from the agency where I get my cleaning work, they've been watching our house and my routes, like checking if I'm taking proper care of my husband. And I do take care of him, I always have—same way I keep a house, you know, you notice every little thing when you clean for a living. But they act like I don't see them watching.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually seen these people watching you or is it more of a feeling that you're being watched?\n\nPatient: I’ve seen enough to know it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling. They act like regular people passing by, but I notice patterns—cars near the house, people around my cleaning routes, and the hospital people asking questions like they already know things. When you clean houses for years, you learn what belongs and what doesn’t, you know? I mean, when the house is quiet, especially by the kitchen, I hear a woman’s voice near the window. Clear, like she’s standing right there, telling me to check the locks or move my husband’s pills. And I know how sounds carry in a house—I’ve cleaned enough places to know pipes, neighbors, all that—but this isn’t like that. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.\n\nAnd I’ve cleaned houses for years, so I know when things don’t fit. Not quite a year, but close. That’s when I started noticing it more clearly, the watching and the things lining up.\n\nAnd the voices too, around that same time. But yes, about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without interrupting or going off on a tangent, just focusing on that one pattern?\n\nPatient: One pattern… okay, I can try. Then it connects to the church schedule, because certain people show up on the same days, and then the numbers on my client list—like house numbers, appointment times—and I know that sounds like I’m jumping around.\n\nPeople tell me they can’t follow me when I explain it. I’m not trying to go off, I just… the words go from one piece to the next before I can slow them down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without explaining how it connects to anything else, just the pattern itself?\n\nPatient: That’s what I’m trying to say… the pattern itself is how my words go. I start with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s in my mind first, and then suddenly I’m talking about the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, like the house numbers or times.\n\nAnd I know you said not how it connects, but that’s where I get stuck, because in my head it doesn’t stay in one little box. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. They say I’m jumping around. I’m not trying to, it just runs from one thing to the next before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me one thing that is happening to you right now, without adding any other details?\n\nPatient: My words are running together right now. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. They say I’m jumping around. I’m trying to keep it to one thing, but they keep wanting to go from the pills to the church schedule to my client numbers, and then people say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that happens a lot. I’ll try to answer one thing, and then my words start moving—like I’ll be talking about my husband’s heart pills, and then I’m already on the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, the house numbers or times.\n\nI don’t mean to do it. People tell me they can’t follow me, and I can see it on their faces sometimes, like I’ve lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts feel jumbled or disorganized to you, or do they make sense in your own mind?\n\nPatient: They make sense to me while I’m saying them, sort of… but then when they come out, they get all tangled. That’s what I mean by patterns. It all connects. They’re watching the house and my routes, trying to see if I’m doing what I’m supposed to do. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. It’s about my husband, whether I’m taking proper care of him. And I’ve worked in people’s homes for years, you notice details—like if someone’s been in a room, or if a car is there too often, or people show up where they don’t belong. So maybe other people can brush that off, but I can’t. That’s why I’m here, because they act like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you connect the hospital people, the agency, and the patterns you've noticed, in a step-by-step way?\n\nPatient: Well… step by step, it’s like, first my husband’s at the hospital and they start asking all these questions, like they already know things about our house. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.\n\nAnd I’ve cleaned houses for years, so I know when things don’t fit. Like one lady I used to clean for, she’d move every little figurine and then ask if I dusted right—so I notice details, you know? People think maids don’t see anything, but we see everything.\n\nSo the hospital people are worried, or pretending to be worried, about whether I’m taking proper care of my husband. And then some agency folks are around my routes, and it matches up. That’s why I say they’re watching the house and watching where I go. I’m here because people keep acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns and feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: About… I think about eleven months. Not quite a year, but close. That’s when I started noticing it more clearly, the watching and the things lining up.\n\nAnd the voices too, around that same time. I mean, it’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first you tell yourself maybe it’s nothing, like when you hear a noise in the house and you think it’s just the pipes or the neighbors. Our old place had pipes that knocked all the time, so I know the difference between normal house sounds and… anyway. But yes, about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without interrupting or going off on a tangent, just focusing on that one pattern?\n\nPatient: One pattern… okay, I can try. It’s like when I start explaining it, I begin with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s what they keep looking at, whether I’m keeping track right. Then it connects to the church schedule, because certain people show up on the same days, and then the numbers on my client list—like house numbers, appointment times—and I know that sounds like I’m jumping around.\n\nPeople tell me they can’t follow me when I explain it. They say, “Stay on one thing,” but it all runs together in my head because it’s all part of the same pattern. I’m not trying to go off, I just… the words go from one piece to the next before I can slow them down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without explaining how it connects to anything else, just the pattern itself?\n\nPatient: That’s what I’m trying to say… the pattern itself is how my words go. I start with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s in my mind first, and then suddenly I’m talking about the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, like the house numbers or times.\n\nAnd I know you said not how it connects, but that’s where I get stuck, because in my head it doesn’t stay in one little box. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. They say I’m jumping around. I’m not trying to, it just runs from one thing to the next before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me one thing that is happening to you right now, without adding any other details?\n\nPatient: My words are running together right now. I’m trying to keep it to one thing, but they keep wanting to go from the pills to the church schedule to my client numbers, and then people say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that happens a lot. I’ll try to answer one thing, and then my words start moving—like I’ll be talking about my husband’s heart pills, and then I’m already on the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, the house numbers or times.\n\nI don’t mean to do it. People tell me they can’t follow me, and I can see it on their faces sometimes, like I’ve lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts feel jumbled or disorganized to you, or do they make sense in your own mind?\n\nPatient: They make sense to me while I’m saying them, sort of… but then when they come out, they get all tangled. Like I start trying to explain one thing, and my words go from my husband’s heart pills to the church schedule, and then to the numbers on my client list.\n\nIn my mind it feels like I’m following it, but other people say they can’t follow me. Then I get embarrassed, because I don’t always know where I lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel organized and it's easy to stay on one topic?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, maybe, but not when I’m trying to explain what’s happening. Then it starts to run off on me. I’ll mean to stay with one thing, and then I’m talking about my husband’s heart pills, and then the church schedule, and then those numbers on my client list… and people tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble staying on one topic when talking to someone, or is this something that has started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: It feels more like it’s been happening lately… or at least people point it out more now. I didn’t used to notice it so much.\n\nLike when I try to explain what’s going on, the words just run off—my husband’s heart pills, then the church schedule, then the numbers on my client list, and I’m still thinking it connects somehow. But then people say, “Wait, what are you talking about?” and I realize they can’t follow me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... I came in because people keep telling me I'm imagining things, but I'm not. The hospital people, and some folks from the agency where I get my cleaning work, they've been watching our house and my routes, like checking if I'm taking proper care of my husband. And I do take care of him, I always have—same way I keep a house, you know, you notice every little thing when you clean for a living. But they act like I don't see them watching.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually seen these people watching you or is it more of a feeling that you're being watched?\n\nPatient: I’ve seen enough to know it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling. They act like regular people passing by, but I notice patterns—cars near the house, people around my cleaning routes, and the hospital people asking questions like they already know things. When you clean houses for years, you learn what belongs and what doesn’t, you know? And now they’re acting like I’m making it up, but I know they’re watching to see if I’m taking proper care of my husband.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes... I mean, when the house is quiet, especially by the kitchen, I hear a woman’s voice near the window. Clear, like she’s standing right there, telling me to check the locks or move my husband’s pills. And I know how sounds carry in a house—I’ve cleaned enough places to know pipes, neighbors, all that—but this isn’t like that. It’s right there by the kitchen window.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by patterns, and how you connect those to people watching you?\n\nPatient: It’s like… when the same kind of car is sitting near our house more than once, or I see somebody from the agency around where I’m cleaning, not where they usually would be. People think I don’t notice, but I do. I’ve cleaned houses a long time, and you learn when something’s been moved or when somebody’s been somewhere they shouldn’t.\n\nAnd then the hospital people ask things about my husband, about whether I’m taking care of him right, like they already have been checking. That’s what I mean by patterns. It all connects. They’re watching the house and my routes, trying to see if I’m doing what I’m supposed to do. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. It’s about my husband, whether I’m taking proper care of him. And I’ve worked in people’s homes for years, you notice details—like if someone’s been in a room, or if a car is there too often, or people show up where they don’t belong. So maybe other people can brush that off, but I can’t. That’s why I’m here, because they act like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you connect the hospital people, the agency, and the patterns you've noticed, in a step-by-step way?\n\nPatient: Well… step by step, it’s like, first my husband’s at the hospital and they start asking all these questions, like they already know things about our house. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.\n\nAnd I’ve cleaned houses for years, so I know when things don’t fit. Like one lady I used to clean for, she’d move every little figurine and then ask if I dusted right—so I notice details, you know? People think maids don’t see anything, but we see everything.\n\nSo the hospital people are worried, or pretending to be worried, about whether I’m taking proper care of my husband. And then some agency folks are around my routes, and it matches up. That’s why I say they’re watching the house and watching where I go. I’m here because people keep acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns and feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: About… I think about eleven months. They say, “Stay on one thing,” but it all runs together in my head because it’s all part of the same pattern. I’m not trying to go off, I just… the words go from one piece to the next before I can slow them down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without explaining how it connects to anything else, just the pattern itself?\n\nPatient: That’s what I’m trying to say… the pattern itself is how my words go. I start with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s in my mind first, and then suddenly I’m talking about the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, like the house numbers or times.\n\nAnd I know you said not how it connects, but that’s where I get stuck, because in my head it doesn’t stay in one little box. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. They say I’m jumping around. I’m not trying to, it just runs from one thing to the next before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me one thing that is happening to you right now, without adding any other details?\n\nPatient: My words are running together right now. I’m trying to keep it to one thing, but they keep wanting to go from the pills to the church schedule to my client numbers, and then people say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that happens a lot. I’ll try to answer one thing, and then my words start moving—like I’ll be talking about my husband’s heart pills, and then I’m already on the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, the house numbers or times.\n\nI don’t mean to do it. People tell me they can’t follow me, and I can see it on their faces sometimes, like I’ve lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts feel jumbled or disorganized to you, or do they make sense in your own mind?\n\nPatient: They make sense to me while I’m saying them, sort of… but then when they come out, they get all tangled. I’ll mean to stay with one thing, and then I’m talking about my husband’s heart pills, and then the church schedule, and then those numbers on my client list… and people tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble staying on one topic when talking to someone, or is this something that has started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: It feels more like it’s been happening lately… or at least people point it out more now. They say they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\n[Last Question]\nWas your thinking always this disorganized, or is this something that has changed over time?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23, F25\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well... I came in because people keep telling me I'm imagining things, but I'm not. The hospital people, and some folks from the agency where I get my cleaning work, they've been watching our house and my routes, like checking if I'm taking proper care of my husband. And I do take care of him, I always have—same way I keep a house, you know, you notice every little thing when you clean for a living. But they act like I don't see them watching.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually seen these people watching you or is it more of a feeling that you're being watched?\n\nPatient: I’ve seen enough to know it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling. They act like regular people passing by, but I notice patterns—cars near the house, people around my cleaning routes, and the hospital people asking questions like they already know things. When you clean houses for years, you learn what belongs and what doesn’t, you know? And now they’re acting like I’m making it up, but I know they’re watching to see if I’m taking proper care of my husband.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes... I mean, when the house is quiet, especially by the kitchen, I hear a woman’s voice near the window. Clear, like she’s standing right there, telling me to check the locks or move my husband’s pills. And I know how sounds carry in a house—I’ve cleaned enough places to know pipes, neighbors, all that—but this isn’t like that. It’s right there by the kitchen window.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by patterns, and how you connect those to people watching you?\n\nPatient: It’s like… when the same kind of car is sitting near our house more than once, or I see somebody from the agency around where I’m cleaning, not where they usually would be. People think I don’t notice, but I do. I’ve cleaned houses a long time, and you learn when something’s been moved or when somebody’s been somewhere they shouldn’t.\n\nAnd then the hospital people ask things about my husband, about whether I’m taking care of him right, like they already have been checking. That’s what I mean by patterns. It all connects. They’re watching the house and my routes, trying to see if I’m doing what I’m supposed to do. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. It’s about my husband, whether I’m taking proper care of him. And I’ve worked in people’s homes for years, you notice details—like if someone’s been in a room, or if a car is there too often, or people show up where they don’t belong. So maybe other people can brush that off, but I can’t. That’s why I’m here, because they act like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you connect the hospital people, the agency, and the patterns you've noticed, in a step-by-step way?\n\nPatient: Well… step by step, it’s like, first my husband’s at the hospital and they start asking all these questions, like they already know things about our house. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.\n\nAnd I’ve cleaned houses for years, so I know when things don’t fit. Like one lady I used to clean for, she’d move every little figurine and then ask if I dusted right—so I notice details, you know? People think maids don’t see anything, but we see everything.\n\nSo the hospital people are worried, or pretending to be worried, about whether I’m taking proper care of my husband. And then some agency folks are around my routes, and it matches up. That’s why I say they’re watching the house and watching where I go. I’m here because people keep acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns and feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: About… I think about eleven months. Not quite a year, but close. That’s when I started noticing it more clearly, the watching and the things lining up.\n\nAnd the voices too, around that same time. I mean, it’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first you tell yourself maybe it’s nothing, like when you hear a noise in the house and you think it’s just the pipes or the neighbors. Our old place had pipes that knocked all the time, so I know the difference between normal house sounds and… anyway. But yes, about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without interrupting or going off on a tangent, just focusing on that one pattern?\n\nPatient: One pattern… okay, I can try. It’s like when I start explaining it, I begin with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s what they keep looking at, whether I’m keeping track right. Then it connects to the church schedule, because certain people show up on the same days, and then the numbers on my client list—like house numbers, appointment times—and I know that sounds like I’m jumping around.\n\nPeople tell me they can’t follow me when I explain it. They say, “Stay on one thing,” but it all runs together in my head because it’s all part of the same pattern. I’m not trying to go off, I just… the words go from one piece to the next before I can slow them down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without explaining how it connects to anything else, just the pattern itself?\n\nPatient: That’s what I’m trying to say… the pattern itself is how my words go. I start with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s in my mind first, and then suddenly I’m talking about the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, like the house numbers or times.\n\nAnd I know you said not how it connects, but that’s where I get stuck, because in my head it doesn’t stay in one little box. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. They say I’m jumping around. I’m not trying to, it just runs from one thing to the next before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me one thing that is happening to you right now, without adding any other details?\n\nPatient: My words are running together right now. I’m trying to keep it to one thing, but they keep wanting to go from the pills to the church schedule to my client numbers, and then people say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that happens a lot. I’ll try to answer one thing, and then my words start moving—like I’ll be talking about my husband’s heart pills, and then I’m already on the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, the house numbers or times.\n\nI don’t mean to do it. People tell me they can’t follow me, and I can see it on their faces sometimes, like I’ve lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts feel jumbled or disorganized to you, or do they make sense in your own mind?\n\nPatient: They make sense to me while I’m saying them, sort of… but then when they come out, they get all tangled. Like I start trying to explain one thing, and my words go from my husband’s heart pills to the church schedule, and then to the numbers on my client list.\n\nIn my mind it feels like I’m following it, but other people say they can’t follow me. Then I get embarrassed, because I don’t always know where I lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel organized and it's easy to stay on one topic?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, maybe, but not when I’m trying to explain what’s happening. Then it starts to run off on me. I’ll mean to stay with one thing, and then I’m talking about my husband’s heart pills, and then the church schedule, and then those numbers on my client list… and people tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble staying on one topic when talking to someone, or is this something that has started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: It feels more like it’s been happening lately… or at least people point it out more now. I know they say I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that maybe, just maybe, you're misunderstanding the situation and these people are not actually watching you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to think that, because everybody keeps saying maybe I’m “misreading” things, but… no, I don’t think so. I know what it looks like when people are just around, and this is different.\n\nThe hospital people and some people from the agency, they’ve been watching our house and where I go for cleaning. It’s about my husband, whether I’m taking proper care of him. And I’ve worked in people’s homes for years, you notice details—like if someone’s been in a room, or if a car is there too often, or people show up where they don’t belong. So maybe other people can brush that off, but I can’t. That’s why I’m here, because they act like I’m imagining it, and I’m not.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you connect the hospital people, the agency, and the patterns you've noticed, in a step-by-step way?\n\nPatient: Well… step by step, it’s like, first my husband’s at the hospital and they start asking all these questions, like they already know things about our house. Then after that I start noticing cars near the house, and people from the agency showing up near places I clean, places they don’t have any reason to be.\n\nAnd I’ve cleaned houses for years, so I know when things don’t fit. Like one lady I used to clean for, she’d move every little figurine and then ask if I dusted right—so I notice details, you know? People think maids don’t see anything, but we see everything.\n\nSo the hospital people are worried, or pretending to be worried, about whether I’m taking proper care of my husband. And then some agency folks are around my routes, and it matches up. That’s why I say they’re watching the house and watching where I go. I’m here because people keep acting like I’m imagining it, but I’m not.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing these patterns and feeling like you're being watched?\n\nPatient: About… I think about eleven months. Not quite a year, but close. That’s when I started noticing it more clearly, the watching and the things lining up.\n\nAnd the voices too, around that same time. I mean, it’s hard to put an exact day on it, because at first you tell yourself maybe it’s nothing, like when you hear a noise in the house and you think it’s just the pipes or the neighbors. Our old place had pipes that knocked all the time, so I know the difference between normal house sounds and… anyway. But yes, about eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without interrupting or going off on a tangent, just focusing on that one pattern?\n\nPatient: One pattern… okay, I can try. It’s like when I start explaining it, I begin with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s what they keep looking at, whether I’m keeping track right. Then it connects to the church schedule, because certain people show up on the same days, and then the numbers on my client list—like house numbers, appointment times—and I know that sounds like I’m jumping around.\n\nPeople tell me they can’t follow me when I explain it. They say, “Stay on one thing,” but it all runs together in my head because it’s all part of the same pattern. I’m not trying to go off, I just… the words go from one piece to the next before I can slow them down.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe one of these patterns you've noticed without explaining how it connects to anything else, just the pattern itself?\n\nPatient: That’s what I’m trying to say… the pattern itself is how my words go. I start with my husband’s heart pills, because that’s in my mind first, and then suddenly I’m talking about the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, like the house numbers or times.\n\nAnd I know you said not how it connects, but that’s where I get stuck, because in my head it doesn’t stay in one little box. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying. They say I’m jumping around. I’m not trying to, it just runs from one thing to the next before I can stop it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me one thing that is happening to you right now, without adding any other details?\n\nPatient: My words are running together right now. I’m trying to keep it to one thing, but they keep wanting to go from the pills to the church schedule to my client numbers, and then people say they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking to someone?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that happens a lot. I’ll try to answer one thing, and then my words start moving—like I’ll be talking about my husband’s heart pills, and then I’m already on the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, the house numbers or times.\n\nI don’t mean to do it. People tell me they can’t follow me, and I can see it on their faces sometimes, like I’ve lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do your thoughts feel jumbled or disorganized to you, or do they make sense in your own mind?\n\nPatient: They make sense to me while I’m saying them, sort of… but then when they come out, they get all tangled. Like I start trying to explain one thing, and my words go from my husband’s heart pills to the church schedule, and then to the numbers on my client list.\n\nIn my mind it feels like I’m following it, but other people say they can’t follow me. Then I get embarrassed, because I don’t always know where I lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts feel organized and it's easy to stay on one topic?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, maybe, but not when I’m trying to explain what’s happening. Then it starts to run off on me. I’ll mean to stay with one thing, and then I’m talking about my husband’s heart pills, and then the church schedule, and then those numbers on my client list… and people tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble staying on one topic when talking to someone, or is this something that has started happening more recently?\n\nPatient: It feels more like it’s been happening lately… or at least people point it out more now. I didn’t used to notice it so much.\n\nLike when I try to explain what’s going on, the words just run off—my husband’s heart pills, then the church schedule, then the numbers on my client list, and I’m still thinking it connects somehow. But then people say, “Wait, what are you talking about?” and I realize they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Did you used to have trouble keeping your thoughts organized when talking to people before the past eleven months?\n\nPatient: Before that? I don’t know… I mean, I could talk normally enough, I think. It’s more now when I try to explain what’s been happening, it just starts running together.\n\nLike I’ll start with my husband’s heart pills, because that matters, and then I remember the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, and to me it feels connected, but people look at me like they lost me. They say they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Was your thinking always this disorganized, or is this something that has changed over time?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it was always like this. I mean, I used to be able to explain things better, I think. Now when I try to tell someone what’s happening, it’s like the words slip sideways—first I’m talking about my husband’s heart pills, then I’m remembering the church schedule, and then those numbers on my client list, because they feel like they fit together in my head. But people stop me and say they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nDoctor: Was your thinking always this disorganized?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think it was always like this. I used to be able to say what I meant better.\n\nNow when I try to explain, it kind of jumps around in my mouth—like I start with my husband’s heart pills, because that feels important, then I’m talking about the church schedule, and then the numbers on my client list, and in my head it all belongs together. But people keep stopping me and saying, “I can’t follow you.”\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I do get upset, because I’m trying to say it straight and it won’t come out that way. Like I’ll start telling somebody about what’s happening, and then the words go off to my husband’s heart pills, and then the church schedule, and then those numbers on my client list—because they seem connected to me, they really do. But then people say, “I can’t follow you,” and that makes me anxious, like I’m failing to explain something important.
